ParlaMint-AT_2000-12-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00050_d7e2336	Ladies and gentlemen! In the course of the session, I have set myself the goal of getting out, as far as possible, without calling for an order. I have also maintained this for a relatively long time – also in the form that, if there have been appropriate statements, which are located beyond the limit of the reasonable, I have had talks with the honourable Member concerned: either at the same time as the Presidency or immediately thereafter. At the last meeting, this border was also exceeded, and I have therefore decided to issue regulatory calls for two extremes today in the sense of a certain degree of uniformity. The first concerns Mrs Partik-Pablé for her interlocutory call "These are Nazi methods from the SPÖ!" [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: Mrs Petrovic can say that from the speaker's desk!]] The second concerns Mr Posch for the expression "Nazi language" during his speech. [[Abg. Dr. Partik-Pablé: But Mrs. Petrovic claimed from the speaker's desk that the "Nazi method" was!]] I will continue to try in the future to deal with calls of order as sparingly as possible. But this also requires the participation of all the Members of this House. I would also like to address a point which was the subject of a lively discussion at the last meeting, namely the argument by Mrs Fekter that Mrs Petrovic has tried to influence the management of the minutes. I had a discussion with the rapporteur immediately after the meeting. She has confirmed to me – and she has also stated this in the Minutes – that Mrs Petrovic has gone to her and has asked her, or has approached her, to include a alleged statement by Mr Schweitzer in the minutes, which the leader of the minutes has not heard. I believe that we all agree that an influence on the management of the protocol is absolutely unacceptable and that we will not do such things in the future. [[Congratulations with the SPÖ, the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I believe that today's day should also be the occasion for adopting a specific, special measure in its own choice of words. With this request, I return the presidency to President Fischer.

Question 4: Of course, we will continue to involve all the social partners in the decision-making process. We will work together to find the best solutions, including external experts. On the question 5, where have we been asked what projects we have carried out to ensure the economic location and jobs: We have set ourselves the goal of sound state finances. We have liberalized electricity and gas markets. We have solved the long-happy question of the fight against money laundering in connection with the savings books. We have improved the capital market and its framework conditions. We have decided on a pension reform that will make pensions safe for the elderly – as an addition: today's pension adjustment of over 5 billion Schilling more for Austrian pensioners, which will be a very large contribution to the fight against poverty in Austria. We have taken the step towards high-performance universities. And we have started with the Public Service Union a very essential way to reform public administration, which is now being implemented step by step. One point I would particularly like to mention, because it is always criticized, is the privatisation strategy. [[Abg. Dr. Jarolim: Explain the strategy to us!]] Consider what actually happened. In the severe crisis of the state-run industry, 1985/1986, the ÖIAG was then restructured. At first, there were successes. Then again was centralized, by Strings and Sekyra, and the Austrian industries were founded. Further billions of losses followed with the AMAG crisis, and then Johannes Ditz and I, then Minister of Economic Affairs, enforced decentralization and privatisation for the first time. There were important stock market introductions, a total of 23 billion shillings of privatisation proceeds in the three years - 1994 to 1996. But – and this is the interesting point – the federal redundancies have been reduced from 46.9 billion to 46.86 billion schilling in this period. That means, the central point was: It has been privatized by more than 20 billion schillings, but the level of ÖIAG debt guaranteed by the federal government has remained completely unchanged. If you call this a right strategy, namely to use privatisation proceeds for the interest payments of debt, I congratulate you, ladies and gentlemen. But that's not our way. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] That's why we drew the consequences. We have merged the ÖIAG, the Post and Telekom. As a result, the debt of the new ÖIAG amounted to 86.5 billion schilling. We have already reduced the debt by over 40 billion schilling within one year! Within a year, we reduced the debt, for which the taxpayer should otherwise have been liable, by over 40 billion schilling. The ÖIAG believes that with the still outstanding reasonable privatisations, PMA, dorotheum, tobacco and Telekom, it can reduce debt to zero until the end of the legislative period. Ladies and gentlemen! That is a reasonable strategy, and that is what we stand for. It is also good for the country and for businesses, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] On question 6: This question relates to the evaluation by the experts, by Austrian and international economic researchers. Of course, the Austrian institutions have regularly reviewed the consolidation programme. In the last forecast, from September, the government's consolidation rate has been absolutely positive. The economic research institutes have said that consolidation is at the right time. The international institutions welcome the course. The EU forecast is set out as follows: Favourite growth prospects despite restrictive tax stands. In its recent Outlook, the OECD assesses the consolidation rate as a positive sign. Ladies and gentlemen! We can be quite satisfied with these points. Edlinger: In the last government you were the driver!]] Finally, 150 billion schilling more debt! Ladies and gentlemen! That means putting the future of the boys at risk – and we don't want to! [[Abg. Dr. Gusenbauer: Offensive you passed over!]] We have made 5.5 billion schilling available to pensioners, as mentioned earlier. Mr. Prime Minister and others have repeatedly stressed that they want to – supposedly! – this "eugenic indication" abolished. However, the facts speak another language, for example, Mr. Minister Chief said in a television discussion that a change in the abortion regime would only be possible if the prenatal diagnostics could be improved. And this, please, I consider in this context a game with sunken cards! [[Congratulation with the greens.]] But now to the situation of women after all these budget cuts. However, because of the short speaking time, I can now only deal with a few points. Women – unfortunately, in the general discussion on employment – are among the so-called "working some" the population group with the highest share. "working some" is the group of workers who, while working, even work very much, but do not deserve enough to live. In this group there are very, very many part-time workers, very many in employment conditions from which they do not acquire any pension rights – and unfortunately there are very, very many women in this group. The current so-called "job miracle" needs to be looked more closely at: In fact, it looks like women's employment is falling in full-time equivalents. The fact that women in Austria are much more often affected by poverty than men and receive less than half of their benefits in old age and invalidity pensions is thanks to a blasphemous fact and decades of social policy among social-democratic social ministers, who have been nominated by the trade union. If the union today calls well-paid AHS teachers to strike and send them to the streets, hundreds of thousands of poverty-affected women and families of this country will probably be as shaken as I am. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-12-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00050_d7e22758	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! A car usually has four wheels and one fifth as a replacement wheel. The sixth wheel is rarely mentioned, although it is actually the most important one, namely the steering wheel. With him we direct the car to the goal, with him we correct deviations and drive obstacles. We have control of cars under control, but we are often overwhelmed by the management of highly complex organizations. Those who want to control must first set goals and define them clearly, otherwise they do not know where they want to go. Health care and its funding are difficult areas to control, because medical progress, the own dynamics of diseases and the related resource needs require responsibility, effective thinking as well as goal-oriented thinking and action. I now ask as a question in the room: Does Social Security Know Responsibility? Does social security aim? - Not really. It is always only about increasing revenue and increasing contributions. That's the only goal the officers have there. In the context of responsibility, I think first of all of the design of contracts with health care providers. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Huber.]] – Mrs Colleague! Only today the contracts with the Red Cross and with established doctors were terminated in the Styria by a health insurance fund, without any reason, only to be renegotiated. Secondly, There is no transparency. Just think of the different rates in the federal states! Why is the caregiver paid less for a Styrian patient than the above Austrian doctor is paid for his patient in his country? – That is inexplicable to me! Thirdly, There is no humanity. Example: wheelchairs. If a wheelchair is provided to a patient in Upper Austria, it will be adjusted to the patient whose weight, size and so on, and the patient will receive an education. In the Styria, on the other hand, the wheelchair is simply put in front of the door. Is that humanity? Ladies and gentlemen! Should not the patient and thus the human being be at the centre of insurance policy? – The patient is currently not at the centre, and, unfortunately, he often does not have the strength to focus on himself because of the state of emergency of his illness. That is why it is our job as a health politician to help him. On the other hand, of course, this is very difficult. The health insurance companies already have a deficit of 5 billion in 2000 and a deficit of 4.95 billion schilling in 2001. Contributions are linked to the total wage, which has increased by 20 percent since 1993. However, health expenditure increased by 27.5%. Since 1993, drug costs alone have exploded by 60 percent. Social security needs fundamental changes in its system. The Deputy Director-General of the Main Association, Josef Probst, says – you're amazed!, I quote literally –: "It cannot be the solution to increase revenue without doing anything in the system. If we don't get to grips with expenditure dynamics, we have a problem again in a few years." - Quote. [[Abg. Silhavy: Who said that?]] - Josef Probst. It's in the "Economic Sheet" today, and I'm surprised! Listen, listen! Probst admits that there can not only be an increase in revenue, but also a need to think about management opportunities in its own field. I congratulate the Minister of Social Affairs and, of course, the Secretary of State for making a rethink at least at the main association of social security schemes. But our Minister of Social Affairs makes the impossible possible!

ParlaMint-AT_2000-12-05-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00050_d7e24737	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! Let me briefly comment on a few points raised. I can begin with the question of the so-called black lists of companies that violate genetic engineering marking rules. As far as the option raised by the opposition is concerned, I would like to agree with it, only the legal bases under the Data Protection Act do not allow such lists. But I am happy to be ready to talk together to find out if there is no way to improve here. As far as the question of genetic engineering as a whole is concerned, I have not been able to provide any specific information in the Committee on the EU's 90/220 decision, because negotiations have just taken place in Brussels at the same time. These are comprehensive regulations in terms of ecologically sensitive areas. Austria is fully involved in this, and the negotiations on this are at the last stage. I would then like to be prepared to organise a joint survey on implementation. On the raised question of the Tortilla chips, where illicit gene manipulation was suspected or could be demonstrated: A number of samples have been drawn which will be evaluated by the end of December and, if they are positive, will be forwarded in the form of an ad to the administrative criminal authority. I agree, as I believe, with all the parties represented in Parliament that there must be a clear commitment to this: Genetic engineering is already taking place in the field of research and medicine, genetic engineering no in the field of food manipulation, especially in the USA, because the side effects of genetically modified foods are becoming increasingly known, particularly in the case of a rapid increase in allergies. As far as the BSE question is concerned, I assume that there has already been a sufficient debate on it today. Unfortunately, this is not widespread in our media. But I heard this on Swedish television this morning, after I already knew it myself. The Right of Warranty Amendment Act also brings changes to the ABGB. One of the points concerns the interaction between traders – consumers. As a result of the Consumer Directive, there is a deterioration for the dealer: a warranty period of two years instead of six months up to now and a return of proof within the first six months. A deterioration for consumers occurs by the fact that, in the case of fixable defects, only the possibility of improvement and no longer a reduction in prices should exist. The question here is whether the person who has already done badly should actually have a second chance. Subject to § 933b para. 1 is the question of whether only the entrepreneur should have the right of access or whether the same should apply to the consumer. As far as the improvement of the special use is concerned, I think this is a very good measure to protect the small dealer, who is located between the consumer and the large dealer multi. I think it is good for him to be able to assert his claims in the distribution chain within an absolute period of five years, and I would like to consider whether we should do something to extend his own claims against the foreman, perhaps the three months available to him from the fulfilment of the claims against him. The next weeks will provide an opportunity to address these and other issues in detail in the right of warranty. I am sure that mothers will release their children to adoption only in the extreme emergency. The adoption of a child is socially accepted. I think it is important that it is also socially accepted that mothers should release their children for adoption under certain circumstances, because adoption without approval is not possible. There are several legal questions to be resolved. I think it is important that we quickly create the legal framework for anonymous care in pregnancy and for anonymous birth, so that the "babynest" remains what it is, a good first step!

However, in the current drug law, § 13 clearly states that a student must be shown drug abuse, a vague suspicion is not sufficient; and even if one-off drug abuse can be demonstrated, the student must not be expelled from school. [[Abg. Dr. Fekter: It is also determined that the school is responsible!]] Mrs Fekter! This is a knowledge of the administrative court! For me, the Federal Law on a school order stands, so I see! That is why I would like to ask the Minister of Justice to deal with this matter and this case again. Perhaps you can interact with the director of this school in this respect, because I believe it is important that two young people get a chance and not be torn out of their training process. That's my concern, and I think that's important. A short while to reduce the limit: Please remember that all drug coordinators vehemently voted against it! Several state governments have also spoken out against it, for example in Lower Austria, Upper Austria, Tyrol, Vorarlberg and Styria – these are the federal states whose country leaders do not belong to the SPÖ. These have warned of the reduction of the limit. I also spoke to the president of a Drug Advisory Council: He said to the planned reduction of the limit: The reduction of the limit for heroin from 0.5 to 0.3 grams excludes people from therapy and will therefore cost human lives. Federal Minister! Think about that again in this sense! You know that this is the limit-rate regulation on 15. It will be decided on December in the main committee. Get off of it! Stay with the current limit! That would save people – I am convinced of that. Thank you.
